{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfiyVqzdGKZQmgQokQsan","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_015LuKFjXpLSe3QwaCfpSie1","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/llmtest/evals/20261002-142720/cahn-hilliard_claude-opus-5.5-cc-medium_hybrid_r4/cahn-hilliard/cahn_hilliard.cpp","content":"// Hybrid MPI + OpenMP + CUDA Cahn-Hilliard benchmark.\n//\n// Parallelization:\n//  - MPI: 1D slab decomposition along z, one rank per GPU. Each rank keeps two\n//    ghost planes of c on each side, so a single halo exchange per time step\n//    suffices (mu is recomputed on the first ghost plane locally).\n//  - CUDA: stencil kernels for chemical potential and update; the halo exchange\n//    (D2H copy -> MPI -> H2D copy) is overlapped with the interior kernels.\n//  - OpenMP: host-side reductions/validation of the gathered field.\n#include <algorithm>\n#include <chrono>\n#include <cmath>\n#include <cstdio>\n#include <cstdlib>\n#include <cstring>\n#include <vector>\n\n#include <cuda_runtime.h>\n#include <mpi.h>\n#include <omp.h>\n\n#include \"../common/results_output.hpp\"\n\n#define CUDA_CHECK(call)                                                                     \\\n    do {                                                                                     \\\n        const cudaError_t err_ = (call);                                                     \\\n        if (err_ != cudaSuccess) {                                                           \\\n            fprintf(stderr, \"CUDA error %s at %s:%d\\n\", cudaGetErrorString(err_), __FILE__, \\\n                    __LINE__);                                                               \\\n            MPI_Abort(MPI_COMM_WORLD, 1);                                                    \\\n        }                                                                                    \\\n    } while (0)\n\n// Physical parameters\nconstexpr double dx = 1.0;\nconstexpr double dy = 1.0;\nconstexpr double dz = 1.0;\nconstexpr double dt = 0.01;\nconstexpr double e_AA = -(2.0 / 9.0);\nconstexpr double e_BB = -(2.0 / 9.0);\nconstexpr double e_AB = (2.0 / 9.0);\nconstexpr double gamma_ = 0.5;\nconstexpr double D = 1.0;\n\nconstexpr int BX = 32;\nconstexpr int BY = 8;\n\n// Laplacian with clamped boundary conditions. `p` points to the cell (x, y, z)\n// of a local slab; z clamping is decided by the global z coordinate.\n__device__ __forceinline__ double laplacian(const double* __restrict__ p, const int x, const int y,\n                                            const long long gz, const int nx, const int ny,\n                                            const long long nz, const long long plane) {\n    const double cc = __ldg(p);\n    const double cxp = __ldg(p + ((x < nx - 1) ? 1 : 0));\n    const double cxn = __ldg(p - ((x > 0) ? 1 : 0));\n    const double cyp = __ldg(p + ((y < ny - 1) ? nx : 0));\n    const double cyn = __ldg(p - ((y > 0) ? nx : 0));\n    const double czp = __ldg(p + ((gz < nz - 1) ? plane : 0));\n    const double czn = __ldg(p - ((gz > 0) ? plane : 0));\n\n    const double cxx = (cxp + cxn - 2.0 * cc) / (dx * dx);\n    const double cyy = (cyp + cyn - 2.0 * cc) / (dy * dy);\n    const double czz = (czp + czn - 2.0 * cc) / (dz * dz);\n    return cxx + cyy + czz;\n}\n\n// c and mu point to local plane 0; computes mu for local planes [zb, ze).\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY)\nchemicalPotentialKernel(const double* __restrict__ c, double* __restrict__ mu, const int nx,\n                        const int ny, const long long nz, const long long z0, const int zb,\n                        const int ze) {\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BX + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BY + threadIdx.y;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny) return;\n    const long long plane = (long long)nx * ny;\n    for (int z = zb + (int)blockIdx.z; z < ze; z += gridDim.z) {\n        const long long idx = (long long)z * plane + (long long)y * nx + x;\n        const double cv = __ldg(c + idx);\n        mu[idx] = 4.5 * ((cv + 1.0) * e_AA + (cv - 1.0) * e_BB - 2.0 * cv * e_AB) + 3.0 * cv +\n                  cv * cv * cv - gamma_ * laplacian(c + idx, x, y, z0 + z, nx, ny, nz, plane);\n    }\n}\n\n// Updates cnew for local planes [zb, ze).\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY)\nupdateKernel(double* __restrict__ cnew, const double* __restrict__ cold,\n             const double* __restrict__ mu, const int nx, const int ny, const long long nz,\n             const long long z0, const int zb, const int ze) {\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BX + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BY + threadIdx.y;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny) return;\n    const long long plane = (long long)nx * ny;\n    for (int z = zb + (int)blockIdx.z; z < ze; z += gridDim.z) {\n        const long long idx = (long long)z * plane + (long long)y * nx + x;\n        cnew[idx] = __ldg(cold + idx) +\n                    dt * D * laplacian(mu + idx, x, y, z0 + z, nx, ny, nz, plane);\n    }\n}\n\n// Initialize local slab (planes [0, nzl)) of the concentration field.\n__global__ void initKernel(double* __restrict__ c, const int nx, const int ny, const size_t vol,\n                           const long long z0, const int nzl) {\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BX + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BY + threadIdx.y;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny) return;\n    const size_t plane = (size_t)nx * ny;\n    for (int z = (int)blockIdx.z; z < nzl; z += gridDim.z) {\n        const size_t linear_id = (size_t)(z0 + z) * plane + (size_t)y * nx + x;\n        const double pseudo = ((((linear_id + 1) * 1299709) % vol) / static_cast<double>(vol));\n        c[(size_t)z * plane + (size_t)y * nx + x] = -1.0 + 2.0 * pseudo;\n    }\n}\n\nstatic dim3 gridFor(const int nx, const int ny, const int nplanes) {\n    return dim3((nx + BX - 1) / BX, (ny + BY - 1) / BY, std::max(1, std::min(nplanes, 65535)));\n}\n\nstatic void launchMu(const double* c, double* mu, int nx, int ny, long long nz, long long z0,\n                     int zb, int ze, cudaStream_t s) {\n    if (ze <= zb) return;\n    chemicalPotentialKernel<<<gridFor(nx, ny, ze - zb), dim3(BX, BY), 0, s>>>(c, mu, nx, ny, nz,\n                                                                             z0, zb, ze);\n}\n\nstatic void launchUpdate(double* cnew, const double* cold, const double* mu, int nx, int ny,\n                         long long nz, long long z0, int zb, int ze, cudaStream_t s) {\n    if (ze <= zb) return;\n    updateKernel<<<gridFor(nx, ny, ze - zb), dim3(BX, BY), 0, s>>>(cnew, cold, mu, nx, ny, nz, z0,\n                                                                  zb, ze);\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<double>& c, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    const size_t n = c.size();\n    // Check for NaN or Inf\n    bool bad = false;\n#pragma omp parallel for reduction(|| : bad)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        if (std::isnan(c[i]) || std::isinf(c[i])) bad = true;\n    }\n    if (bad) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    // Check if values are in reasonable range for concentration field\n    // After Cahn-Hilliard evolution, values should typically remain bounded\n    double minVal = c[0];\n    double maxVal = c[0];\n#pragma omp parallel for reduction(min : minVal) reduction(max : maxVal)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        minVal = std::min(minVal, c[i]);\n        maxVal = std::max(maxVal, Each rank keeps two\n//    ghost planes of c on each side, so a single halo exchange per time step\n//    suffices (mu is recomputed on the first ghost plane locally).\n//  - CUDA: stencil kernels for int BY = 8;\n\n// Laplacian with clamped boundary conditions. `p` points to the cell (x, y, z)\n// of a local slab; z clamping is decided by the global z coordinate.\n__device__ __forceinline__ double laplacian(const double* __restrict__ p, const int x, const int y,\n                                            const long long gz, const int nx, const int ny,\n                                            const long long nz, const long long plane) {\n    const double cc = __ldg(p);\n    const double cxp = __ldg(p + ((x < nx - 1) ? 1 : 0));\n    const double cxn = __ldg(p - ((x > 0) ? 1 : 0));\n    const double cyp = __ldg(p + ((y < ny - 1) ? nx : 0));\n    const double cyn = __ldg(p - ((y > 0) ? nx : 0));\n    const double czp = __ldg(p + ((gz < nz - 1) ? plane : 0));\n    const double czn = __ldg(p - ((gz > 0) ? plane : 0));\n\n    const double cxx = (cxp + cxn - 2.0 * cc) / (dx * dx);\n    const double cyy = (cyp + cyn - 2.0 * cc) / (dy * dy);\n    const double czz = (czp + czn - 2.0 * BY)\nchemicalPotentialKernel(const double* __restrict__ c, double* __restrict__ mu, const int nx,\n                        const int ny, const long long nz, const long long z0, const int zb,\n                        const int ze) {\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BX + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BY + threadIdx.y;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny) return;\n    const long long plane = (long long)nx * ny;\n    for (int z = zb + (int)blockIdx.z; z < ze; z += gridDim.z) {\n        const long long idx = (long long)z * plane + (long long)y * nx + x;\n        const double cv = __ldg(c + idx);\n        mu[idx] = 4.5 * ((cv + 1.0) * e_AA + (cv - 1.0) * e_BB - 2.0 * cv * e_AB) + 3.0 * cv +\n                  cv * cv * cv - gamma_ * laplacian(c + idx, x, y, z0 + z, nx, ny, nz, plane);\n    }\n}\n\n// Updates cnew for local planes [zb, ze).\n__global__ void __launch_bounds__(BX * BY)\nupdateKernel(double* __restrict__ cnew, const double* __restrict__ cold,\n             const double* }\n}\n\n// Initialize local slab (planes [0, nzl)) of the concentration field.\n__global__ void initKernel(double* __restrict__ c, const int nx, const int ny, const size_t vol,\n                           const long long z0, const int nzl) {\n    const int x = blockIdx.x * BX + threadIdx.x;\n    const int y = blockIdx.y * BY + threadIdx.y;\n    if (x >= nx || y >= ny) return;\n    const size_t ((((linear_id + 1) * 1299709) % vol) / static_cast<double>(vol));\n        c[(size_t)z * plane + (size_t)y * nx + x] = -1.0 + 2.0 * pseudo;\n    }\n}\n\nstatic dim3 gridFor(const int nx, const int ny, const int nplanes) {\n    return dim3((nx + BX - 1) / BX, (ny + BY - 1) / BY, std::max(1, std::min(nplanes, 65535)));\n}\n\nstatic void launchMu(const double* c, double* mu, int nx, int ny, long long              long long nz, long long z0, int zb, int ze, cudaStream_t s) {\n    if (ze <= zb) return;\n    updateKernel<<<gridFor(nx, ny, ze - zb), dim3(BX, BY), 0, s>>>(cnew, cold, mu, nx, ny, nz, z0,\n                                                                  zb, ze);\n}\n\nbool validateResult(const std::vector<double>& c, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nx, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t ny, [[maybe_unused]] const size_t nz) {\n    const size_t n = c.size();\n    // Check for NaN or Inf\n    bool bad = false;\n#pragma omp parallel for reduction(|| : bad)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        if (std::isnan(c[i]) || std::isinf(c[i])) bad = true;\n    }\n    if (bad) {\n        printf(\"Validation failed: found NaN or Inf value\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    //    printf(\"Validation failed: values out of expected range\\n\");\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    return true;\n}\n\nvoid printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s               const double dx, const double dy, const double dz, const size_t x, const size_t y, const size_t z) {\n    const size_t xp = (x < nx - 1) ? x + 1 : x;\n    const size_t yp = (y < ny - 1) {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011Cfiz2meJCCgecxiySd7yV","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The benchmark now runs as a hybrid MPI + CUDA + OpenMP program. I only changed `cahn_hilliard.cpp` and `CMakeLists.txt`, and the executable is still `cahn_hilliard`. Results match the original exactly when both are built without FMA, and it runs about 50× faster on one RTX 3090 than the serial code.\n\n**How it's split up:**\n- **MPI:** the grid is cut into slabs along z, one rank per GPU; ranks on the same node are spread across that node's GPUs. Each rank keeps two extra planes of `c` from each neighbour. That way it can compute `mu` one plane beyond its own slab, and only one halo exchange is needed per time step instead of two.\n- **CUDA:** the initialisation, chemical-potential and update steps run as GPU kernels. While the boundary planes are being exchanged (GPU → host → MPI → GPU), the GPU keeps working on the inner planes, which don't need that data. The full field is gathered to rank 0 only when `-r` or `-v` is given.\n- **OpenMP:** the validation checks on rank 0 (NaN/Inf, min/max) run in parallel.\n- **Edge cases:** if there are fewer than two z-planes per rank, the extra ranks just sit idle. Exit codes and the output format are unchanged.\n- **CMake:** it now needs CMake 3.24 or newer. The source is compiled with nvcc for the local GPU type, and it links MPI, OpenMP and the CUDA runtime.\n\n**Correctness:**\n- While testing I found and fixed a race: a halo receive could overwrite a buffer that was still being copied to the GPU, which gave different results from run to run with 4 ranks.\n- Built with `-DCMAKE_CUDA_FLAGS=-fmad=false`, the result hash is identical to the original built with `-ffp-contract=off`. I checked 1, 3 and 4 ranks on several grids, including odd sizes and uneven slabs.\n- The default build lets nvcc fuse multiply-adds (FMA), just as your original g++ `-O3 -march=native` build already does. The last bits therefore depend on the compiler: GPU and CPU fuse different operations, so even the original's hash changes with that flag. With FMA on, the results are identical across 1–8 ranks and across repeated runs, and validation passes.\n\n**Performance** (512³, 100 steps, default build):\n\n| GPUs | MCellUpdates/s |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | 8,270 |\n| 2 | 16,130 |\n| 4 | 30,500 |\n\nThe original serial build gets about 168. I only changed `cahn_hilliard.cpp` and `CMakeLists.txt`, and the executable is still `cahn_hilliard`. Results match the original exactly when both are built without FMA, and it runs about 50× faster on one RTX 3090 than the serial code.\n\n**How it's split up:**\n- **MPI:** the grid is cut into slabs along z, one rank per GPU; ranks on the same node are spread across that node's GPUs. Each rank keeps two extra planes of `c` from each neighbour. That way it can compute `mu` one plane beyond its own slab, and only one halo exchange is needed per time step instead of two.\n- **CUDA:** the initialisation, chemical-potential and update steps run as GPU kernels. While the boundary planes are being exchanged (GPU → host → MPI → GPU), the GPU keeps working on the inner planes, which don't need that data. The full field is gathered to rank 0 only when `-r` or `-v` is given.\n- **OpenMP:** the validation checks on rank 0 (NaN/Inf, min/max) run in parallel.\n- **Edge cases:** if there are fewer than two z-planes per rank, the extra ranks just sit idle. Exit codes and the output format are unchanged.\n- **CMake:** it now needs CMake 3.24 or newer. The source is compiled with nvcc for the local GPU type, and it links MPI, OpenMP and the CUDA runtime.\n\n**Correctness:**\n- While testing I found and fixed a race: a halo receive could overwrite a buffer that was still being copied to the GPU, which gave different results from run to run with 4 ranks.\n- Built with `-DCMAKE_CUDA_FLAGS=-fmad=false`, the result hash is identical to the original built with `-ffp-contract=off`. I checked 1, 3 and 4 ranks on several grids, including odd sizes and uneven slabs.\n- The default build lets nvcc fuse multiply-adds (FMA), just as your original g++ `-O3 -march=native` build already does. The last bits therefore depend on the compiler: GPU and CPU fuse different operations, so even the original's hash changes with that flag. With FMA on, the results are identical across 1–8 ranks and across repeated runs, and validation passes.\n\n**Performance** (512³, 100 steps, default build):\n\n| GPUs | MCellUpdates/s |\n|---|---|\n| 1 | 8,270 |\n| 2 | 16,130 |\n| 4 | 30,500 |\n\nThe original serial build gets about 168.
